#2949. Why CleanSpark Stock Was a Winner on Wednesday

The company published its final earnings report for fiscal 2026.

By Eric Volkman – Nov 25, 2026 at 7:10PM EST

10 stocks we like better than CleanSpark ›

CleanSpark's (CLSK +1.27%) final earnings release of its current fiscal year was published after market hours on Tuesday. The following day, investors showed their somewhat cautious appreciation for the company's performance by trading its shares up by almost 1.3%. While not every metric in CleanSpark's fourth quarter was impressive, the company improved several important line items.

We can't say that for net revenue, as it declined to $120.9 million from $223.7 million in the fourth quarter of 2025. That wasn't a shock or surprise, however, as the company's legacy business of Bitcoin (BTC +2.31%) mining was affected by a drop in the crypto's average price while it was building out its artificial intelligence (AI) data center business.

The dynamic was much different on the bottom line, where CleanSpark flipped to a net profit of $9.8 million ($0.04 per share) from the year-ago loss of $925,000. Much of this, however, was tied to its legacy business -- it booked a nearly $306 million accounting gain on the value of its Bitcoin, despite the digital currency's recent softening.

This meant a mixed quarter for CleanSpark, as it whiffed on the analyst consensus estimate of $134.3 million. Yet it trounced the average pundit expectation of a $0.33 per share net loss.

The company's results are choppy due to the nature of the Bitcoin mining business, which, for obvious reasons, depends on the cryptocurrency's price. CleanSpark has now entered a pivotal fiscal year for its transformation, as it expects to begin drawing revenue from high-profile clients for AI data center space.

Given that, I'd say that investors were buying into CleanSpark based on that high-potential future rather than the unexpected flip into profitability. I'd be even more cautious than those folks. At the same time, though, AI data center operation is almost certainly a durable and lucrative business; this company isn't the only legacy crypto miner making the transition, and it hasn't yet proven it can soar above such peers.

#2948. 4 Ways to Prep Your Finances Before the Next Tech Outage

We believe everyone should be able to make financial decisions with confidence. While we don't cover every company or financial product on the market, we work hard to share a wide range of offers and objective editorial perspectives.

So how do we make money? Our partners compensate us for advertisements that appear on our site. This compensation helps us provide tools and services - like free credit score access and monitoring. With the exception of mortgage, home equity and other home-lending products or services, partner compensation is one of several factors that may affect which products we highlight and where they appear on our site. Other factors include your credit profile, product availability and proprietary website methodologies.

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

You don’t need a basement bunker, but keeping some cash on hand might help.

If your social media feed is anything like mine, you’ve probably seen a reel or two on nonperishable food and solar-powered generators after the recent string of tech outages.

Have I considered giving water-filtering straws as stocking stuffers this year? Maybe.

But what about your money? If your area was hit by a natural disaster or your bank had an outage, are your finances ready?

Dan Stanush, senior financial planner at Charles Schwab in Asheville, North Carolina, recalls how fast financial conveniences disappeared after Hurricane Helene hit in 2024. “We lost power for three weeks and water for six weeks,” he said in an email. “And some local businesses could only accept cash or checks because card systems and ATMs were down.”

Don’t get caught unprepared. Here are the moves experts suggest to help you weather various types of emergencies:

Last week’s Chase outage was a good reminder that access to your bank balance can be disrupted. Stash at least enough cash at home — in small bills — to cover one week of expenses, says Jeff Judge, a certified financial planner (CFP) with Chesapeake Financial Planners in Forest Hill, Maryland.

If systems go down, ATMs can go down with them, said Eileen Olson, a CFP with Emerald Wealth in Daufuskie Island, South Carolina, in an email. Keeping cash in a safe at home is a good plan, but it’s also smart to carry cash when you travel. (Remember when planes were grounded nationwide a week and a half ago?)

“My parents and sister were traveling overseas on 9/11 and got stuck much longer than they expected,” Olson said. “After that, they always traveled with more cash.”

If your primary bank and its app were down, would you be able to pay bills? Having an account at more than one bank gives you an alternate funding source. Consider opening a high-yield savings account at a second bank or credit union for your emergency fund.

“This is the step I push hardest,” Judge says. “I had a client whose debit card, credit card and savings all ran through one bank, so when that bank’s system went down, she couldn’t even buy gas.”

If you don’t want to open a second bank account, a second credit card from a different bank and preferably on a different network (i.e. Visa vs. Mastercard) can provide another way to pay for things. “If there’s a problem with one credit card network, I have a backup with two other credit cards,” says Tony Steuer, author of “Get Ready! A Step-by-Step Planner for Maintaining Your Financial First Aid Kit.”

Make a printed list of your accounts, along with account numbers and key contact numbers. Include your bank, insurers, credit card companies and any financial professionals. Store the list with copies of essential paperwork, including insurance policies and their declarations pages, estate planning documents, legal certificates and identification — potentially in a fire-resistant file box.

Keep a digital copy of your documents in a password-protected, encrypted spot as well. A password manager can store all of your login info.

Joon Um, a CFP with Secure Tax & Accounting in Beverly Hills, California, suggests digital storage site Everplans to his clients, but there are a variety of sites that offer encrypted document storage.

Take some time to write down how you’d pay for groceries, gas and medication if your usual payment methods weren’t available. What expenses would you need to cover first? What recurring bills are on autopay? Where would you keep the cash and any key documents you’d want to take with you if you had to leave home? Make sure other household members know where everything is.

And set a reminder to update your records at regular intervals. Steuer, for instance, saves his account statements as PDFs and prints everything out once a year. “One of the big concerns is that people could conceivably break into your 401(k) and drain your 401(k) account,” he says. Account statements could help if a hack affects any financial balances.

It all adds up to a web of protection that (hopefully) you’ll never have to use.

“The goal is not to anticipate every scenario,” said Schwab's Dan Stanush, “but to have enough flexibility to meet basic needs while systems come back online.”

#2946. Cross-Functional Collaboration Is Fashion’s New Competitive Advantage

Maximilien Abadie, Deputy CEO and a member of the Executive Committee, Lectra.

​Speed and creativity have always been defining features of the fashion industry. In a climate marked by fragmentation and continuing volatility, accelerating time to market and achieving the all-important five R’s (right place, right product, right price, right quantities and right consumer) have become less about moving faster at individual stages and more about improving how information is shared and decisions are made across the value chain.

Companies that create the digital and cultural infrastructure to support cross-functional collaboration make it possible to unify workflows, anticipate challenges earlier and share data for faster, better decisions. This transforms the way a company creates, manufactures and sells their products. ​

Every product that makes it to market is shaped by contributions from hundreds of different individuals, many of whom do not speak the same language or share a common perspective. The linear, step-by-step approach to collection development—design followed by development, then production and ending with marketing, merchandising and e-commerce—struggles to provide the agility needed to respond quickly to the dynamism that characterizes this industry.

When design, production and sales teams work as separate entities, each with their own tools, data and objectives, decision-making is slower and inefficiencies are greater. A feasibility issue discovered in production could have been identified at the development stage. Consumer insights gathered during the marketing phase are not passed on to creative teams. These disconnects can result in missed sales opportunities, excess inventory and frustrated customers. ​

Cross-functional collaboration supported by shared data contributes to greater resilience, innovation and growth. Research shows that cross-functional, team-focused transformations can drive a 30% increase in efficiency. When information flows freely across an interconnected value chain, decisions happen faster, errors are identified earlier and fewer resources are wasted.

One of the most tangible benefits of data-based collaboration is its impact on time to market, which remains a critical challenge for global fashion brands. While some fashion players can develop and launch products in a matter of days, others still operate on timelines of six to nine months. Closing this gap requires rethinking product development from the earliest creative stages and ensuring continuity of information across the entire product life cycle.

Beyond improving collaboration, these solutions help preserve and scale expertise by making institutional knowledge, technical know-how and historical product data accessible across the organization. Along with accelerating onboarding and decision-making, this helps brands ensure that valuable expertise is retained and leveraged across generations of teams.

Data-based collaboration requires a shared infrastructure capable of integrating multiple functions and partners. Solutions embedded with AI and digitalization are the key enablers of this transformation. While product life cycle management (PLM) solutions remain essential for product data management, governance and life cycle visibility, a new generation of AI-enabled product development solutions is emerging to support the day-to-day workflow of bringing a product from concept to production.

By bringing together internal teams, as well as a brand’s global network of suppliers, subcontractors and partners, these solutions ensure that all stakeholders are aligned and working from the same up-to-date information. Companies can capture and communicate data on designs, tech packs, materials, costs, timelines and suppliers in a common framework that cross-functional teams can rely on as products progress.

When each team member has a 360-degree vision of the product in its journey, there are fewer surprises and improved coordination.

The case for shared data infrastructure becomes even more urgent in light of upcoming traceability and sustainability regulations. The Digital Product Passport (DPP), part of the European Union’s Ecodesign for Sustainable Products Regulation, will require products sold in the EU in 2027 to carry structured, standardized product information. Even in the early phase of implementation, which will be a minimal, simplified version of the digital product passport, companies will be expected to provide data on material composition, origin, carbon footprint, supply chain traceability, recyclability, care, durability and chemical compliance.

Compliance will require alignment across the full product cycle. While the first phase will most likely only require supply chain traceability up to Tier 1 and Tier 2 suppliers, adopting a digital traceability approach that starts with the original fibers that will eventually be used in garments and other products provides the foundation for visibility across all tiers of the supply chain. Artificial intelligence is a key enabler of this tracing technology. It continuously monitors transactions to detect inconsistencies or fraudulent claims, enabling brands to move from theoretical commitments to proven actions. Companies that build this infrastructure now will be positioned to leverage transparency as a competitive advantage.

Collaborative solutions are reshaping how work is organized, executed and experienced. Beyond enabling basic communication, they support new ways of working that emphasize shared visibility and consistency across teams. In a business environment increasingly characterized by volatility, complexity and rapid change, these platforms are becoming an important enabler for companies seeking to operate more efficiently and quickly.

#2944. Vertex Pharmaceuticals Has Turned $10,000 Into $61,660 Over 10 Years. Here's What Could Come Next

Vertex's next growth wave is already in the pipeline.

By Prosper Junior Bakiny – Nov 25, 2026 at 7:12PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Vertex Pharmaceuticals ›

Vertex Pharmaceuticals (VRTX +0.04%), a biotech company, has been on an impressive run over the past decade. If you had invested $10,000 in the stock in 2016, you'd have about $61,660 today. By comparison, $10,000 invested in the S&P 500 10 years ago would be worth about $38,663 today (with dividends reinvested) -- that's not bad, but it pales in comparison to the returns you would have had with Vertex. That said, the past is the past. The important question is whether the drugmaker can deliver similarly impressive returns going forward.

Vertex has crushed broader equity markets thanks to its dominance in the cystic fibrosis (CF) market. The company has very little competition in this niche, and thanks to several new launches over the past 10 years that have allowed it to treat increasingly more CF patients, revenue and earnings growth have been strong. Vertex's CF franchise should remain an important growth driver over the next decade. After all, the company's most important medicines won't lose patent exclusivity until the late 2030s.

But in the meantime, Vertex should significantly diversify and expand its lineup. The company's work in kidney diseases looks particularly promising. Vertex Pharmaceuticals could earn approval for one product in its kidney disease portfolio, povetacicept -- a medicine for IgA nephropathy -- by the end of the month. This medicine has peak sales potential of around $4 billion across IgA nephropathy and other potential indications, according to some estimates.

Of course, that outcome isn't guaranteed yet, not by a long shot. But the point is that Vertex's kidney disease pipeline, with products like povetacicept and others, including inaxaplin and VX-407, looks promising. The biotech leader could, eventually, generate close to $10 billion in peak sales from these products if all goes well. Another area where Vertex could make progress is pain treatments.

It has already earned approval for Journavx, a medicine for acute pain and the first oral non-opioid pain signal inhibitor ever. Journavx is in several ongoing clinical trials and could earn key label expansions, while Vertex works on other pain treatments. Further, thanks to a recent acquisition, Vertex Pharmaceuticals has an interesting endocrine diseases portfolio that it believes could reach more than $5 billion in annual sales.

We haven't even mentioned other newer launches, including Casgevy, a gene-editing medicine for a pair of rare blood diseases. Vertex's potential across its entire pipeline is massive, and even after probability-adjusting sales projections, since pipeline products aren't guaranteed approval, my view is that the company's prospects look promising. That said, I wouldn't expect Vertex Pharmaceuticals to repeat its performance since 2016 over the next decade.

That's a tall order for a larger biotech that faces more competition than it did 10 years ago. Even so, from current levels, Vertex is well positioned to beat the market over the long run, making it a great stock to buy.

#2943. When Days Matter: Navigating The AI-Accelerated Exploit Lifecycle

Eoin Keary Founder & CEO of Edgescan, an enterprise CTEM (Continuous Testing and Exposure Management platform) used by Fortune 500s.

​AI is fundamentally changing the velocity of the threat landscape, particularly the time between vulnerability discovery and weaponization. Vulnerabilities that historically took months, or even up to a year, to become active exploits can now move into the wild within days. AI also expands the attack surface itself as organizations deploy AI systems and agents across their environments, including systems that may not be fully visible to security teams.​

This creates a new imperative: Security processes designed around periodic assessment and remediation must evolve to operate at the speed of continuous change. They should consider a different approach to security that employs AI to manage data volume while retaining a human in the loop for critical decision making. In this way, their organizations can rise with the tide of AI-driven threats by purifying their cybersecurity data to ensure it remains manageable and actionable.​

The fundamental challenge in vulnerability management has never been finding vulnerabilities but determining which findings represent genuine, actionable risk. Organizations must distinguish between false positives, vulnerabilities that are real but not exploitable and vulnerabilities that are exploitable but mitigated by other controls.​

Only after those layers of validation can security teams determine which remaining vulnerabilities represent the greatest business risk and should be addressed first. AI threatens to amplify the existing “vulnerability noise” problem by dramatically increasing the volume of findings entering the security team’s workflow. The answer can’t be asking already-constrained security and development teams to manually process an increasingly powerful “firehose” of alerts.​

Slowing down detection isn’t the answer. A better answer is to add a validation layer between detection and action. The value of continuous assessment is its ability to provide a more current view of the environment. Without validation, however, it just produces more noise, faster. This is especially important as AI accelerates both vulnerability discovery and vulnerability exploitation.

The temptation to use AI to automate remediation and allow systems to patch vulnerabilities without human intervention is obvious.​ Yet autonomous remediation can introduce significant operational risk when an AI system lacks the business context necessary to understand the consequences of its actions. AI should be used to make security professionals “more bionic,” processing information, accelerating analysis and supporting decisions, while humans retain responsibility for critical remediation decisions.​

As for technical vulnerability data, it alone is insufficient to determine what an organization should fix first. Effective prioritization requires context about the affected asset, its criticality, its role in the business, its data flows and the potential consequences of exploitation. AI can help organizations apply and process this context at scale, but only if the underlying security data is accurate, validated and meaningful.

For teams already buried in findings, the promise of less noise is appealing. The questions start when we discuss what adopting it involves. How much of their time will we need? Will it work with their existing tools? Will the findings arriving on their desks actually be any better?

In conversations with customers and prospective clients, these are always understandable concerns. They’ve heard plenty of promises about making security simpler. They want to know how much work it’ll take to get started and how much work it’ll save them afterwards. Sending over a mountain of alerts and wishing the team good luck is a fairly generous interpretation of helping.

Providers need to be clear about who does the work. If we offer validated findings, the validation is our responsibility. It’s important to establish which findings are real and investigate exploitability and assess relevant controls before passing the results to the customer.

Trust is another hurdle. Fewer findings can sound suspicious when the industry has spent years presenting bigger numbers as evidence of a better job. Customers need to see why something has been ruled out or deprioritized, what testing supports that decision and what remains uncertain.

“AI said so” isn’t an explanation I’d fancy taking into a board meeting. Show the evidence and let people challenge the conclusion.​

Yes, cost always comes up, but the subscription is only part of the calculation. Investigating false positives, chasing findings that can’t be reproduced and passing inconclusive tickets between teams all costs money. Those hours don’t become free because they’re missing from the procurement spreadsheet. Providers must demonstrate the reduction in effort for the client and explain the price they charge.

The practical route is to start with a defined scope and agree what success looks like with security development and the budget owner. Measure the time spent investigating findings, the quality of evidence reaching developers and how quickly confirmed risks receive attention. Make implementation manageable, work with existing systems and show the results. That gives each team a reason to support adoption.​​

Organizations need greater visibility into their attack surface and a continuous understanding of what they’re trying to secure. Continuous assessment and visibility will become increasingly important as the pace of vulnerability discovery and exploitation accelerates. However, continuous assessment inevitably creates more data, making validation and suppression of irrelevant or low-value findings increasingly important.

Organizations should prioritize security approaches that continuously assess the environment while filtering, validating and contextualizing findings before they reach security and development teams. The ultimate objective isn’t to automate every security decision but to ensure that even as the volume of cybersecurity data grows exponentially, organizations can reduce that information to a manageable number of accurate, contextual and actionable risks.​

In an AI-accelerated threat landscape, the organizations best positioned to respond will be those that can rise with the tide by purifying their cybersecurity data rather than drowning in it.​​

#2942. Broadcom Stock Hasn't Traded This Low Since July 2025. I Think It's a Buy.

The AI chip designer's stock now prices in very little of next year's planned growth.

By Daniel Sparks – Nov 25, 2026 at 7:43PM EST

Broadcom (AVGO -0.07%) stock fell about 5% on Wednesday to under $290 as I write, on a day when chip stocks sold off broadly. The slide took the chipmaker to its lowest point since July 2025, even below the $291.53 close it hit on Nov. 5. Shares are now about 40% under June 2's record close of $481.57.

The business is going the other way. Management's September guidance was for 93% year-over-year revenue growth in its fiscal fourth quarter of 2026 (the period that ended Nov. 1, 2026). That would be an even faster rate than the quarter before.

At its lowest price in over a year, is Broadcom a buy? I think so. Today, investors are paying for very little of Broadcom's artificial intelligence (AI) growth. What gives me pause is how few companies that growth relies on.

Broadcom's revenue growth has accelerated sharply in the last year. For its fiscal third quarter (the period that ended Aug. 2, 2026), sales climbed 86% year over year to $29.6 billion, up from 22% growth the year before. Most of the gain was from AI chips. AI semiconductor revenue more than tripled from the year before, hitting $16.7 billion, or 56% of the total. Non-GAAP (adjusted) earnings per share jumped 96% to $3.32, and free cash flow grew 95% to $13.7 billion.

Management expects more. Its forecast calls for fiscal 2027 AI revenue of about $115 billion, twice its expected fiscal 2026 total. And on the September earnings call, CEO Hock Tan said Broadcom has already lined up the supply to get there.

If Broadcom's fiscal fourth quarter lands where management guided (revenue of $34.8 billion with an adjusted operating margin of 66%), adjusted earnings would be roughly $3.80 per share by my math. Four quarters at that pace would total about $15 per share.

Today's price, under $290, is about 19 times these earnings. The S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.25%), for comparison, traded at about 17 times forward earnings in FactSet's Nov. 20 report.

Put another way, even if Broadcom's profits never grew past that quarter's pace, the stock would cost only slightly more than the market on a price-to-earnings basis. And if AI revenue doubles next year as planned, earnings should climb well past that pace.

With the earnings per share analysts expect on average for fiscal 2027, the price-to-earnings ratio falls to around 15.

I suspect that low price-to-earnings ratio has a lot to do with how few buyers drive Broadcom's growth. The company estimates in its latest quarterly filing that its five largest end customers made up around half of its revenue in the first three quarters of fiscal 2026.

"[I]n some ways, I'm so glad we have only 6 customers to deal with," Tan said on the September call, talking about the buyers of Broadcom's custom AI chips. He'd just laid out the shortages, from power to memory, that control when a customer's AI data center can come online.

For shareholders, though, this short list is the main risk. As of Aug. 2, Broadcom had committed to buy around $127 billion in supplies, mainly inventory, and its filing warns that customers might cancel or delay orders over issues like a lack of capital or delayed AI build-outs.

Broadcom also disclosed in October that it's backstopping part of the financing for one customer's AI racks. Its maximum potential liability is around $43 billion, but it thinks the chances of having to pay are low. If one big customer cuts back, Broadcom might feel it in its sales and in these commitments at the same time.

Broadcom stock dropped 12% on Nov. 20. That morning, analyst Ming-Chi Kuo of TF International Securities posted that his industry survey found Google, owned by Alphabet, cutting its planned 2027 shipments of one Broadcom-designed line of its custom AI chips by around 20%. Neither company has confirmed that.

But Tan said in September that four of the six customers, Google included, are financially strong enough to fund their build-outs themselves. And in April, Broadcom disclosed a long-term deal to develop and supply Google's future generations of custom AI chips. Tan said Broadcom plans to ship tens of billions of dollars in chips to Google each year for the next several years.

Because investors are paying so little for next year's growth, I think Broadcom stock is a buy below $290. But with so much of fiscal 2027 riding on six customers, I'd start with a small position and build it up gradually.

#2939. Rob Pelinka believes the Lakers are feeding off Luka Dončić’s leadership

Behind Luka Dončić's wondrous play, which has thrust him into the center of early season MVP conversations, the Los Angeles Lakers are off to a roaring 15-3 start and tied for the West's No. 1 seed. Whether it's his elite self-creation, willingness to embrace more off-ball work, brilliant playmaking or growth on the defensive end, there are many on-court factors explaining why Dončić has been so impactful for Los Angeles this year.

But Lakers GM Rob Pelinka also believes Dončić's leadership is paramount to this team's success thus far. Pelinka deems Dončić's leadership as bred through plenty of personal experiences that have shaped him in recent years.

"Adversity in life is what makes us all grow and he's been through a lot. Getting traded, he's had some family stuff, making sure he can stay close to his daughters," Pelinka said prior to Friday's win over the Sacramento Kings. "That's all adversity and I've seen it really grow his character in the form of leadership just being able to go through some of that stuff that life throws all of us, it throws our way. The guys in the locker room feed off his leadership. The other thing I've really seen about Luka is he takes accountability. He has so many great games but if he has a down game, he looks around the guys, he's like, 'This one's on me. I've got to be better.' I think a great leader can recognize that and really have seen that growth in him this year."

Pelinka's mention of accountability was readily on display last week when the Lakers' nine-game winning streak came to a halt after they lost to the Utah Jazz. That night, Dončić scored 29 points on 10-of-28 shooting (2-of-12 from deep) and had seven assists to nine turnovers. Following the defeat, Dončić didn't mince words about his own play and refused to let the second leg of a back-to-back be any sort of excuse for the team's poor performance.

"I mean, honestly, I just got to be way better than this," Dončić told reporters. "This is unacceptable for my part. I've got to lead the team in better ways. I look at this game, got to move on and learn from this."

The Lakers will hope both Dončić's leadership and on-court splendor power them to a win Friday night when they face the San Antonio Spurs in a clash between 15-3 teams vying for the West's top spot.

#2931. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2928. 19 Business Upgrades To Maximize The Value Of Real-Time Payments

As instant-payment networks such as FedNow and the RTP network reach more financial institutions, more businesses have the opportunity to send and receive funds within seconds, even on weekends and holidays. However, taking full advantage of that speed requires more than gaining access to a new payment network. Legacy workflows built around batch processing and traditional banking hours can create delays and operational gaps even when money moves instantly.

For many companies, the transition to real-time payments will require a fresh look at the internal technology and business practices that support financial operations. Below, Forbes Technology Council members share ways companies can modernize their systems and processes to make real-time payments more efficient, secure and beneficial to the business.

Update ERP payment reconciliation and the capacity behind it. From my experience leading fintech integration projects, connecting payment channels is only part of the work. Internal systems must confirm transactions, update balances and flag duplicates at any hour. Adopt API-driven, automated reconciliation with exception handling, and load-test it at peak volume without maintenance windows. - Mohammad Sharif Daoud, Global Center for AI Excellence

Combine automated matching with clear ownership of exceptions. Routine transactions should reconcile automatically, while unmatched payments, short payments and missing remittance details are routed to accountable teams for timely resolution. This approach reduces manual effort, improves visibility into available cash, and helps finance teams put incoming funds to work sooner while resolving discrepancies faster. - Venus Garg, Elevance Health

Forbes Technology Council is an invitation-only community for world-class CIOs, CTOs and technology executives. Do I qualify?

Providers and participants in the expanding instant payments ecosystem must modernize risk assessment alongside payment infrastructure. Instant settlement leaves little room for error, while added verification and manual review undermine speed. Richer financial data and streamlined fraud, liquidity and other risk assessments can enable more instant payments without sacrificing sound controls. - Brian Reshefsky, EDGE

Companies should modernize their treasury and cash management systems. Real-time payments mean little if reconciliation, liquidity management and reporting remain outdated. Integrating automation, AI-driven forecasting and real-time financial visibility enables businesses to optimize cash flow, reduce risk and make faster, smarter capital decisions. - Paul Kerslake, N3XUS

Payment routing deserves more attention. Many companies choose how to send money based on fixed bank relationships or manual processes. Connecting internal systems to multiple payment networks lets them route each payment based on cost, availability and whether the recipient can receive it instantly. That makes real-time payments useful across the business instead of within a single network. - Maghnus Mareneck, Cosmos Labs

I’d modernize how procurement negotiates supplier payment terms. If a company can pay immediately upon verified delivery, procurement should be able to negotiate something in return, such as a discount, better pricing or priority access to inventory. That requires procurement and finance to agree on when using cash sooner is worthwhile. The opportunity is to make payment speed part of the negotiation. - Kacee Johnson, radical

Fraud detection and payment authorization need to evolve with real-time payments. When money moves instantly, there’s little time to detect fraud or reverse a transaction. Companies need real-time fraud analysis and stronger verification that a payment was genuinely authorized by the customer, without slowing down legitimate transactions. - Alex Spokoiny, Check Point Software

Update cash and liquidity management. When payments settle around the clock, including weekends, treasury can’t rely on end-of-day positions and cutoff times. Modernize to real-time cash visibility and automated funding of accounts so you can pay and collect any time without tying up idle balances. - Aruna Veerappan, Upwork Global Inc.

Customer-facing billing and refund workflows should be modernized to take full advantage of real-time payments. Many companies still rely on multi-day settlement, batch disbursements and manual refund processes that frustrate customers accustomed to instant transactions. Modernized workflows deliver instant payouts, real-time refunds and transparent payment status, improving customer experience and loyalty. - Nitin Agarwal, Luminace

Companies should modernize how they track incoming payments. What’s the point of receiving money in seconds if accounting takes two days to recognize it? It’s like getting a package delivered instantly but waiting until tomorrow to open the door. Real-time payments need real-time accounting. - Margarita Simonova, ILoveMyQA

Companies should modernize their payment approval workflows to keep pace with instant-payment networks. If a supplier payment still waits until the next business day for approval, the money may move instantly, but the business hasn’t gained much. Setting approval limits and delegating authority in advance lets routine payments go through around the clock, while larger or unusual transactions still get reviewed before funds leave the account. - Elton Chan, Second Talent

Modernize the payment decisioning and exception-management architecture. Real-time settlement atop batch-era fraud controls, liquidity monitoring, reconciliation and exception handling merely accelerates operational risk. 24/7 money movement demands 24/7 intelligence, controls and accountability. Speed without trust is a liability. - Rajjie Sarmey, FutureProof CXO™

Companies should modernize their liquidity orchestration layer, not just payment gateways. Real-time payments require real-time intelligence across treasury, fraud detection, reconciliation and cash forecasting. The next frontier is agentic liquidity management, where AI continuously anticipates cash needs, dynamically allocates funds and optimizes working capital. Instant settlement should enable instant financial decisions. - Nicola Sfondrini, PWC

Real-time payments won’t automatically create value. I’d challenge the premise that modernizing one process or internal system is enough. Companies need to assess technical debt, process debt and data debt together. Money can move instantly while the business operates on yesterday’s information. The question leaders need to focus on is whether the entire organization can access and act on that data. - Neda Nia, Stibo Systems

Modernize how you approve and double-check payouts before money goes out the door. Older payment methods gave finance teams until the end of the day to catch a duplicate invoice, a wrong account or a bad refund before the bank processed it. With instant payments, the money is gone in seconds, so your approval rules and mistake checks have to happen up front, before anyone clicks “Send.” - Vatsal Bhardwaj, Jabali.ai

Update collections and customer communication. If payment status reaches the CRM overnight, customers who paid at 9 a.m. still get an overdue reminder at noon. At one client, about one in 10 dunning emails went to accounts that had already paid. Streaming payment events into Salesforce stopped those messages, cut “I already paid” support cases by roughly 35%, and released credit holds the same day. - Sergii Grushai, Peeklogic

Modernize tax determination and allocation. When a customer pays instantly, the company should reserve sales tax, VAT and withholding by jurisdiction and legal entity. Many firms calculate these obligations later from batch reports, overstating available cash and rushing remittances. Link payment events to tax rules and entity records, then test reversals and partial payments so speed does not create a compliance backlog. This clearly separates cash from tax owed. - Jagadish Gokavarapu, Wissen Infotech

Your customer service layer needs modernization. Here’s why: Real-time payments are final. There’s no recall window. So the “Here’s my money, can I cancel it?” questions spike, and they need answers in seconds, not tomorrow. In our banking data, payments and money movement drive roughly 36% of guided-task volume. Instant money needs instant, accurate answers. - Merlin Bise, Inbenta

Companies should integrate instant-payment networks directly with their ERP or through treasury platforms, ensuring cash inflows and outflows are reflected in real time. Payment initiation, status updates, and automated AR and AP matching can enable straight-through processing, reduce manual intervention and improve cash visibility. This aligns financial operations with payment speed, improving efficiency and working capital management. - Eranda Maldeniya, Enterprise Analytics

#2925. Kevin Porter Jr. reportedly finalizing 2-year deal with Magic

Kevin Porter Jr. is finally on a team for the 2026-27 NBA season. A month after being waived by the Milwaukee Bucks, Porter will reportedly sign a two-year deal with the Orlando Magic, per ESPN.

Porter, 26, underwent knee surgery in April and was waived by the Bucks just days before the first game of the regular season.

Porter — whose 2025-26 season was delayed due to a knee injury — was out to a great start with the Bucks last season, averaging 17.4 points and 7.4 assists. But he was limited to just 38 games after sustaining another knee injury, this one forcing him to undergo surgery in April.

Porter picked up a player option to remain with the Bucks and was able to take part in Milwaukee's training camp. He appeared in the team's preseason game against the Indiana Pacers just prior to his release, scoring 11 points in 15 minutes.

Despite proving he was healthy, Porter remained on the free-agent market for over a month before reportedly joining the Magic. He also met with the Denver Nuggets during the season.

Once he signs, Porter will join his fifth NBA team since being drafted in the first round of the 2019 NBA Draft.

Porter has had some off-the-court issues that have contributed to his vagabond NBA journey, most notably pleading guilty to misdemeanor assault and a harassment violation in New York in 2024 in a case involving former girlfriend and ex-WNBA player Kysre Gondrezick.

#2921. monday.com's 2027 Outlook: Scaling Enterprise Segment to Drive Sustainable Revenue Growth

This under-the-radar software stock boasts a Superscore of 90 from our Hidden Gems Primary database, part of The Motley Fool's Moneyball Database system. Here's why.

By Robert Izquierdo – Updated Nov 25, 2026 at 9:27PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Monday.com ›

A monday.com (MNDY +2.60%) customer deploying over 87,000 seats is the kind of massive, enterprise-grade scale that would have been unthinkable for the company just a few years ago. Once a simple project-tracking tool for small teams, the company has evolved into a cloud-native "Work OS" where organizations build bespoke applications for everything from CRM to software development. As of November 19, 2026, the stock trades at $70.81, reflecting a 53% decline over the past year as the market grapples with a transition toward AI-integrated workflows and a more mature, slower-growth phase.

Our proprietary Hidden Gems scoring system assigns monday.com an overall Superscore of 90 out of 100, placing it in the Exceptional category. The Superscore is an AI-powered score that evaluates a company's overall strength by combining financial performance, product market position, technological capabilities, leadership quality, and relative valuation. It represents the unification of all our scores into a single score for public companies, with five rating bands: Exceptional (90-100), Strong (75-89), Above Average (60-74), Average (40-59), and Cautious (0-39).

A 90 Superscore places the company in the Top ~1% of all companies we score, which is ahead of roughly 99 out of every 100 companies in our database. This score is one data-driven signal worth investigating, and this article pairs the reasons the score is high with the risks that prevent it from being higher so you can weigh both sides before doing more work.

The Hidden Gems Superscore reflects The Motley Fool's proprietary AI-driven evaluation of a company across product, financial, leadership, and valuation pillars as of the article date and may change over time. Performance figures are point-in-time. Past performance does not guarantee future results.

You may want to keep researching before buying if...

The Superscore is one data-driven signal worth investigating, not a stand-alone buy recommendation, and you should weigh it against your own research, financial goals, and risk tolerance before acting.

Like many software-as-a-service (SaaS) companies this year, monday.com was seen as exposed to structural shifts in how software is bought. The rise of artificial intelligence means fewer employees need to perform manual work in favor of using AI agents, making monday.com's seat-based pricing vulnerable to erosion. Fewer seats purchased equals lower revenue.

The company is taking action to mitigate this threat. It adopted hybrid pricing, where customers can continue to buy seats and also purchase usage-based credit packages for its AI offerings. The approach is working. In Q3, ARR for its AI tools hit $36 million, showing that customers are willing to pay based on usage.

Moreover, monday.com is successfully moving beyond small businesses to land larger, stickier enterprise contracts. Customers with over $500,000 in ARR was up 58% year over year in Q3. This trend bodes well for its long-term business growth.

While its trailing P/E ratio is not cheap, it's at its lowest level in the past year, suggesting now is a good time to consider buying shares compared to historical levels. Given monday.com's pivot to hybrid pricing, its solid product that is producing growing revenue, and its transition to profitability, the company looks poised for business expansion in the coming years.

#2908. Is Nvidia Stock a Buy After Dell's Record AI Backlog?

Most of the server maker's newest AI orders run on the chip giant's latest system.

By Daniel Sparks – Nov 25, 2026 at 9:47PM EST

Dell Technologies (DELL +0.60%) has a record $132.3 billion in artificial intelligence (AI) server orders it hasn't shipped yet. The server maker reported that backlog with its fiscal third-quarter results after the market closed on Tuesday, Nov. 24. Three months before, the total was $95 billion.

The report came after a tough day for Nvidia (NVDA -3.91%) shareholders. Nvidia's shares lost almost 6% on Nov. 24, giving back some of a 31% gain the day before, when the company said its board had reinstated Jensen Huang as CEO. They closed around $155, about 35% under the $239.24 record close they set on Oct. 6. Dell's results came out after the bell, so none of that drop was a response to them.

Dell builds full AI server racks, many of them around Nvidia's chips. That makes its order book one of the more useful outside checks on demand for Nvidia's hardware. And I think it suggests that demand is still growing.

In its fiscal third quarter of 2027, which ended Oct. 30, 2026, Dell booked $60.2 billion of AI orders, while its AI server revenue grew 291% year over year to a record $22 billion. With orders coming in at almost three times the rate of shipments, the backlog kept climbing. It's now over seven times the $18.4 billion Dell posted a year before. At the third quarter's shipping pace, it covers around six quarters of AI server revenue.

Orders slipped a bit from the fiscal second quarter's record $60.9 billion, so the record this time was in the backlog. But it was still Dell's second straight quarter with over $60 billion of AI orders. Dell also lifted its full-year forecast for AI server revenue to $78 billion, up from the $74 billion it expected three months before.

Dell doesn't break out how much of its backlog is built on Nvidia's chips. It also sells servers based on Advanced Micro Devices hardware, so not every dollar belongs to Nvidia.

"The proof point is our orders momentum, $180 billion over the past four quarters, with a majority of our new orders based on Nvidia's Vera Rubin NVL72," said Jeff Clarke, Dell's vice chairman and chief operating officer, in his prepared remarks for the earnings call.

Vera Rubin NVL72 is a full rack of Nvidia's latest AI hardware, and Nvidia started shipping it in its fiscal 2027 third quarter, which ended Oct. 25, 2026. So most of Dell's latest orders are for the product Nvidia is ramping up right now.

Nvidia's revenue from AI clouds, industrial companies, and enterprises hit $47.5 billion that quarter. That almost tripled from $16.1 billion a year before and climbed 18% from the previous quarter.

On Nvidia's Nov. 17 earnings call, Colette Kress, the company's chief financial officer, credited some of the sequential growth to Vera Rubin shipments to top neoclouds (specialized cloud companies that rent out AI computing power). Dell counts neoclouds among its AI server customers, too, along with governments and other businesses.

On the same call, Kress raised Nvidia's forecast for revenue growth in fiscal 2028 (which ends in January 2028) to around 75%, from 70%.

The share price doesn't seem to show much of that confidence. At around $142 on the morning of Nov. 25, after another drop of about 8%, Nvidia's stock costs around 8.5 times earnings, using analysts' average estimate for fiscal 2028. For comparison, FactSet put the forward price-to-earnings ratio of the S&P 500 at around 17 as of Nov. 20, based on expected earnings for the next 12 months.

Put another way, investors are paying about half the S&P 500's forward price-to-earnings multiple for a company forecasting 75% growth. Some of that discount might still come from Huang's Nov. 11 resignation and the uncertainty it caused. But it also suggests many investors doubt the growth will last.

A backlog isn't revenue yet, of course. Customers need financing and data centers with enough power before Dell can deliver, so Dell says it screens every order for access to capital, land, and power.

"We've had no customers reschedule business," Clarke said on the call, answering an analyst.

Is Dell's record backlog enough reason to buy Nvidia stock?

Not by itself. But Dell's numbers, along with Nvidia's raised forecast for fiscal 2028, point to demand for Nvidia's latest systems that's still rising, even with the stock well below its October record. At this price, I'd consider buying the stock.

#2907. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. #2887. HP: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 25, 2026 at 11:10PM EST

Commercial hardware demand and the adoption of artificial intelligence helped offset consumer market softness and rising component costs. HP returned over $2.3 billion to shareholders through dividends and share repurchases in fiscal year 2026. Three takeaways from the call stand out for long-term investors.

Refillable tank hardware placements increased total customer lifetime value and lifted the printing segment's operating margins above target levels.

Component inflation and soft personal computer demand create near-term friction, but structural cost reductions and expanding enterprise adoption of edge computing create a clearer path to profit recovery. Management relies on disciplined pricing, software attach, and commercial workstation growth while modernizing global supply chains.

Free cash flow generation supports consistent shareholder returns, including an 11th consecutive annual dividend increase. Enterprise customers migrating artificial intelligence tasks locally provide HP a durable structural tailwind.

#2884. Where the Dallas Mavericks stand in the NBA cup

After Tuesday night's win over the Denver Nuggets, the Dallas Mavericks are in great position to play for the third annual NBA cup.

The Mavericks are 3-0 in group play, with a point differential of 36, by far the highest in their group.

They also hold the head-to-head tiebreaker over the Denver Nuggets and Houston Rockets. The only team that could jump the Mavericks is the Phoenix Suns, who play the Rockets tonight, and the Mavericks on Thursday.

With a Rockets win, the Suns would be eliminated from contention, and the Mavericks would be in regardless of Friday's result.

If they do advance to the tournament, it would mean a matchup against another western conference contender. This would give the Mavericks another opportunity to show their mettle, and establish themselves as a real threat within the West.

"It means a lot," said Caleb Martin in his post-game interview with Jeff Wade. "Just to have an oppurtunity to play in these types of enviroments… to test where we're at."

While the NBA cup isn't the same as real playoff basketball, it's much closer than the regular season.

It would be especially helpful for a young Mavericks team that has limited experience in the postseason; Cooper Flagg being the player with the most to gain.

And for those questioning the value of cup games, look no further than last year's Cup final, which featured the eventual NBA finals matchup.

It also gives the Mavericks a chance at redemption for their recent 3-game losing streak against other contending teams, and gives them a chance to add more proverbial skins on the wall.

Their seeding within the tournament will come down to Friday's game against the Suns, but they will likely have a home game in the quarterfinal due to their record and point differential.

Rising portfolio yields drive fixed income returns higher, providing steady cash flow as primary market rates moderate. Specialty underwriting discipline and selective capital deployment maintain portfolio margins without chasing market volume.

#2877. Game Preview: Surging Suns look to stuff Rockets on Thanksgiving Eve

Who: Phoenix Suns (9-7) @ Houston Rockets (13-5)

It appears the Phoenix Suns may be figuring things out. The chemistry, camaraderie, and tenacity expected of this team at the start of the season might be materializing after a slow start. The Suns have won all four games on their current road trip and eight of their last ten overall.

After a big win against the Denver Nuggets to kick off the five-game excursion, they took care of business against the Sacramento Kings, Utah Jazz and New Orleans Pelicans, who are a combined 10-45.

Even with the current string of wins, the Suns still sit in 9th place, one spot ahead of the Oklahoma City Thunder, but they are just two losses out of third.

If the Suns can pull off an upset on the road, they might be able to put together an impressive winning streak. After the Rockets game and a home match against the Dallas Mavericks on Friday, the Suns will face the Jazz, Los Angeles Clippers and Kings. Substituting the Clippers for the Pelicans makes this group's cumulative record 9-43. Even if the Suns just split the next two matches, then continue to punish the dregs of the Western Conference, they will stand at 13-8 after a 1-5 start.

They will have their hands full tonight against the Houston Rockets, a matchup that garners extra attention since Durant was traded in a deal that brought back Jalen Green, Dillon Brooks, and a draft pick used to select Khaman Maluach.

Houston is coming off a 116-104 win at home against the Clippers on Monday night. Prior to that, they split a four-game road trip. The Rockets are 8-1 at home this season.

Mark Williams — OUT (Left Shoulder Surgery)

Miles Bridges has been solid for the Suns so far this season but has looked more comfortable in the flow of the game recently. Among the ten rotation players this season, Bridges is first in rebounding, second in free-throw percentage, second in minutes, third in assists, and third in scoring. Looking at advanced statistics, he is first in VORP and second in BPM.

Collin Gillespie leads the team in made three-pointers (37) and has hit at least two in each of his last eight games. It is becoming more apparent that the Suns have their answer at point guard and that coach Jordan Ott needs to hand those reins to the smart, gritty kid from Villanova.

Those two helped spearhead a small-ball lineup that could become a great situational weapon. Along with Booker, Green, and Brooks, this group has shown incredible shooting and playmaking. This lineup still struggles with rebounding but has offset that with effort and basketball IQ.

Those rebounding woes will be put to the test tonight. The Rockets allow the fewest opponent rebounds in the league. The rebounding disparity between the teams is somewhat staggering. The Rockets are +6.0, and the Suns are -4.8 rebounds per game. The team is aware of this glaring problem. Center Khaman Maluach told Arizona Sports 98.7 FM that it has been an area of emphasis in practices and something that he specifically needs to work on. Maluach currently leads the team in rebounds per possession.

At age 38, Kevin Durant is scoring the fewest points per game (21.6) since his rookie season…19 years ago. For comparison, Suns center Maluach is just 20 years old. While he may be slowing down, he is still a deadly shooter. He currently boasts a TS% of .654, which would be the third-highest of his career. The Rockets are led by center Alperen Sengun, who is averaging 21.9 points and 9.2 rebounds per game.

This season's previous matchup on November 7 saw the Suns topple the Rockets 102-99 at Mortgage Matchup Center in Phoenix. Booker led all scorers with 24 points, including the last nine points for the Suns in the game, and the Suns' bench outscored the Rockets' reserves 34-15.

The Suns have a different vibe now than they did then, when the victory pushed the team's record to 3-5. My feelings of "this can't work" have changed to "let's see where this is going." Some of the missing chemistry and effort we had expected right out of the gate has seemed to percolate into a style of basketball that has been much more entertaining after the miserable start. The Suns may not be world beaters, but they are stringing together wins while they appear to be figuring some things out.

#2869. NBA plans to invest 10bn euros in European arenas

The NBA has stepped up its plans for a European league by announcing a strategy to invest at least 10bn euros in the development and renovation of basketball arenas across the continent.

The league has assembled a group of investment firms for the project, which would also look to improve the sports and entertainment districts around the arenas.

The initiative forms part of the NBA and the International Basketball Federation's (Fiba) plan to launch a new, professional basketball system in Europe in 2027.

It will provide prospective clubs and ownership groups with access to capital and the NBA's expertise in regard to developing modern arenas and their surroundings.

"We've seen first-hand how state-of-the-art arenas and the related developments around them can transform basketball teams and their surrounding communities," said NBA commissioner Adam Silver.

"As we prepare to launch a new league in Europe alongside Fiba, we see a significant opportunity to help clubs develop facilities which both build on the unique traditions that define European basketball while also elevating the fan experience and attracting many more live events."

The NBA's announcement stated that the plans for the areas surrounding arenas could include housing, workplaces, restaurants, retail and public spaces, ensuring they would be year-round destinations.

In the statement, the investors said: "We're excited to work with the NBA to support a transformative phase of infrastructure development across Europe.

"Together, we have the potential to invest billions of euros to create world-class venues in European cities, honouring the passion and traditions of European basketball fans and supporting lasting economic growth in the communities where the game means so much."

The announcement cited a study by The Sports Consultancy, which claimed the NBA and Fiba's league could contribute more than 24bn euros in gross value added to GDP across Europe.

It also claimed the league could support nearly 15,000 full-time equivalent jobs annually and attract nearly 70 million spectators over its first 10 years.

#2867. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.
