#2820. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2818. The Aaron Rodgers ultimatum that sent the Steelers into chaos

Aaron Rodgers ended up on the wrong side of his own ultimatum.

The now-former Steelers quarterback was waived by the team on Wednesday, following a wild 18-hour period that saw him get benched in a late-night announcement and then go nuclear on coach Mike McCarthy in a nationally televised tirade.

It all started with a Tuesday meeting between the coach and QB, who won a Super Bowl together in 2011 with the Packers.

"I didn't want this to be the way that it ended," Rodgers said in an explosive appearance on the "Pat McAfee Show" on Wednesday. "But I was very clear with the ultimatum and it wasn't a veiled ultimatum. It wasn't posturing. It was me as a leader of the football team inspired by my teammates who talk about this all the time. Literally all the time in council meetings, on group chats, in the locker room, just some of the things that are just like wow, how is this the reality we're dealing with? I was, in my professional opinion based on 22 years of experience … the only person who could talk to him and be honest with him to tell him that what's best for the team is for him to not call the plays and if he wasn't up for that, they should probably release me."

According to Netflix NFL insider Tom Pelissero, McCarthy was open to Rodgers' demand, as the veteran has been calling plays himself in recent weeks, but other coaches and "some in the locker room" convinced him it was not a move they wanted.

McCarthy confirmed in a press conference later in the day that Rodgers attempted to put the screws to him.

Mike McCarthy and Aaron Rodgers speak on the sidelines as the Steelers face the Bengals. Getty Images

"We got to a point, we talked some things — you know communication and things like that — and there was an ultimatum made clearly outside of his job description and so it was my decision to bench him," McCarthy told reporters.

McCarthy made the call to move backup Mason Rudolph into the starter's role, kicking off a chaotic day in which Rodgers accused his coach of falling asleep in meetings and divulging a halftime gaffe in which he said, "1-2-3 Packers!" among other things.

Pittsburgh players reportedly watched Rodgers burn it all down on McAfee's live ESPN broadcast on their phones in the locker room in real time.

Hours later, the team granted the soon-to-be 43-year-old quarterback his ultimatum and sent him packing.

The Green Bay Packers receiver exited with a thigh injury after making his first catch of the game. He returned on the Packers' second drive. Watson appeared to be in the clear, until taking a huge hit late in the first half from the Los Angeles Rams' Kamren Kinchens.

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2799. RJ Luis suing SEC and Greg Sankey after LSU basketball eligibility fight

The saga that's engulfed LSU, the Tigers' men's basketball program and the SEC has taken yet another turn.

And, to the surprise of no one, it involves lawyers.

RJ Luis, the one-time LSU men's basketball player whose eligibility case has been a point of contention for weeks with the SEC, has filed a lawsuit against the SEC and commissioner Greg Sankey seeking damages caused by the conference's efforts that prompted LSU's decision not to play him following a previously awarded court injunction, acccording to a motion and other documents provided by one of Luis' attorneys to The Daily Advertiser, a part of the USA TODAY Network.

REQUIRED READING: LSU played chicken with the SEC — and finally blinked. Was it worth it? | Opinion

It's unclear how much Luis is seeking in damages.

Luis, who had previously signed NBA contracts with the Utah Jazz and Boston Celtics, was granted two separate injunctions in Louisiana courts, the first on Aug. 3 and another Nov. 13, as he sought eligibility for the 2026-27 college basketball season. Luis committed to play for Tigers coach Will Wade in May.

While Luis was cleared to play by the judicial system, he faced an additional obstacle with the SEC, which in August passed a rule barring member institutions from having athletes compete for them who had previously declared for the NFL, NBA or WNBA drafts.

Following the Nov. 13 injunction, the SEC issued a statement saying it would abide by the court's order and not punish LSU for putting Luis on its roster, but the league mentioned nothing about the Tigers actually playing Luis. On Nov. 18, however, Luis played 15 minutes, scoring 11 points and pulling down nine rebounds, in his team's 79-67 victory against Louisiana Monroe.

One day later, LSU president Wade Rousseau released a statement saying he instructed Wade not to play Luis, who has since been removed from the LSU roster and hasn't played in the team's two most recent games.

That didn't mark the end of the escalating tensions between LSU and the league of which it's a founding member. The SEC's chancellors and presidents are scheduled to hold a vote on Dec. 3 over whether to expel the Tigers from the conference. A two-thirds vote from the 15 non-LSU members is required for the action to pass.

A 6-foot-7 guard/forward, Luis averaged 18.2 points and 7.2 rebounds per game for St. John's during the 2024-25 season, which culminated with him being named Big East player of the year and earning All-American honors. He declared for the 2025 NBA Draft, but was not selected by a team.

#2793. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

Frank Cappelleri is the founder and president of CappThesis, an independent research firm that helps active investors through time-tested chart and statistical analysis. Prior to starting CappThesis, he spent 25 years on Wall Street as an equity sales trader, technical analyst, research sales specialist and desk strategist.

#2790. Rams S Kam Kinchens ejected; Packers WR Christian Watson evaluated for concussion

In the second quarter of the Green Bay Packers-Los Angeles Rams game, Rams safety Kam Kinchens was ejected for leaving his feet and leading with his helmet on a tackle attempt on Packers receiver Christian Watson. You can find video of the play below:

Initially, a cart came out for Watson, but the receiver was able to leave the field under his own power. The Packers are currently listing Watson as questionable to return and say that he's being evaluated for a concussion. To be honest, it looks like the biggest hit he ended up taking was when his head hit the turf on the way down, not necessarily Kinchens' blow to Watson's facemask.

Green Bay was not awarded a first down on this play due to offsetting penalties. Packers receiver Matthew Golden was called for offensive pass interference (you can only set a pick within a yard of the line of scrimmage), so they simply replayed the fourth-and-one play. After not getting a man coverage look with their running back's motion, like they had on the play that Watson was injured on, head coach Matt LaFleur called a timeout and elected to kick the football, setting up a 17-17 tie going into halftime.

We'll keep you updated when we hear more news on the Packers' injury situation.

Update: The Packers have now officially ruled Watson out with a concussion.

#2789. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2786. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guidance; Extension of Comment Period

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

#2775. Federal Reserve Board releases results from survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released results from a survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances. The Senior Financial Officer Survey is used by the Board to obtain information about banks' reserve balance management strategies and practices, their expectations around the size of their balance sheets and deposit balances their activity in money markets, and their views on other matters, such as operations on weekends and holidays.

The survey was conducted in collaboration with the Federal Reserve Bank of New York between September 18, 2026, and September 28, 2026, and includes responses from banks that held around three-fourths of total banking system reserve balances at the time of the survey.

For media inquiries, please e-mail [email protected] or call (202) 452-2955.

#2773. Federal Reserve announces College Fed Challenge winners

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The University of California, Los Angeles won the 23rd Annual National College Fed Challenge.

The University of California, Los Angeles (UCLA) won the 23rd annual national College Fed Challenge on Friday, a team competition that encourages undergraduate students to learn about the U.S. economy, monetary policymaking, and the role of the Federal Reserve System. Teams analyze economic and financial conditions and formulate a monetary policy recommendation, modeling the Federal Open Market Committee. The team, from Los Angeles, California, represented the San Francisco District and included Aarushi Maheshwari, Annika Chan, Atul Prasanth, Caitlyn Cox, Finn Karish, Eli Brooks (alternate), Kennedy Yang (alternate), Manasi Vartak (alternate), Ronojoy Borpujari (alternate), and Sahil Sudani (alternate). The team's advisers were Chris Surro and Ali Haider Ismail.

"I am very happy to congratulate the students who competed in this year's College Fed Challenge, especially the winners and finalists," said Federal Reserve Chairman Kevin Warsh. "They took on the same big questions we policymakers face. I'm impressed by their talent, their rigor, and their eagerness to think hard about monetary policy and the economy."

This year's College Fed Challenge included 141 schools from 34 states across the nation, the most in the competition's history. Teams submitted video presentations or participated in local competitions last month. Twenty-one semi-finalist teams then participated in question-and-answer sessions held earlier this month, and seven finalists were invited to the Federal Reserve Board for the national finals. The other national finalists were the University of Chicago in second place and the University of Maryland, College Park in third place. Teams with honorable mentions include Cleveland State University, Harvard College, Pennsylvania State University, and St. Lawrence University.

Teams were evaluated on their economic analysis, responses to the judges' questions, teamwork, and presentations.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

#2771. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2768. What we learned as De'Anthony Melton's sharp shot fuels Warriors' win vs. Kings

What we learned as De'Anthony Melton's sharp shot fuels Warriors' win vs. Kings originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SAN FRANCISCO – Thanksgiving dinner came early for the Warriors on Wednesday night as they feasted on the Sacramento Kings' defense early to enjoy a big enough lead that lasted throughout the game.

The Warriors scored just 88 points Sunday against the Boston Celtics. They then scored half of that in the first quarter against the Kings in an eventual 129-110 win at Chase Center.

After leading the Kings 44-19 through one quarter, the Warriors lost the final three quarters 91-85.

Overall, the Warriors shot 53.3 percent from the field, made 20 threes and tallied 37 assists with just 15 turnovers.

De'Anthony Melton led the way with 24 points, tying his career high of eight 3-pointers in a game. Melton in the Warriors' previous six games had made a total of eight threes.

This was not a one-man show, though. The Warriors had seven players score in double figures, and they won the bench battle 61-19 behind Gui Santos' 22 points.

Here are three takeaways from the Warriors' second straight win.

They play different positions and have much different roles, but whenever Yaxel Lendeborg plays another top pick making a name for himself, eyes instantly will turn to a rookie showdown. On Wednesday night, that meant pinning him against Kings rookie point guard Darius Acuff Jr.

The start of the game wasn't pretty for Lendeborg. Within the first four minutes, Lendeborg already airballed a three, was called for traveling and an offensive foul. He was replaced by Santos after the offensive foul, who instantly drained a 3-pointer.

However, Lendeborg did have three steals in the first quarter and was up to 11 points at halftime while Acuff was held to four.

Though Acuff (22 points) had more points than Lendeborg (15 points), the Warriors rookie had a much more efficient night. Lendeborg was a plus-8 thriving at the cup, as a cutter and screening for others, and Acuff was a minus-23, going a lowly 9 of 24 from the field and 3 of 12 beyond the arc.

The wait is over. Brandon Williams has been itching to make his return from a mild hamstring strain and immediately made an impact off the bench.

Williams watched the Warriors dominate the first quarter, and quickly took advantage of the Kings' lack of defense. On the first play of the second quarter, Williams used a ballscreen from Charles Bassey and a backscreen from Will Richard to blow by his defender and finish at the rim. He then went coast to coast, zooming past the Kings for another layup on the Warriors' next possession.

After a strong start, Williams missed his next four shots, failing to finish at the rim. A blocked shot on Keegan Murray got him going again in the third quarter, as Williams quickly converted on the other side.

Some rust had to be scraped off Williams throughout the night. His 14 points in 13 minutes and ability to attack the Kings' defense also played a big role in the Warriors' bench outscoring them by 42 points.

As the Warriors felt multiple times during their recent winless five-game road trip, this game was over in a hurry. It's a four-quarter game, but the doors were closed after the first quarter in favor of the Warriors. Melton catching fire from deep early was a big reason why.

His 3-point shooting was a huge plus to start the season as Melton had a 52.8 3-point percentage (19 of 36) through the first seven games of the season. Melton then went 0 of 9 over his next two games and has struggled to find his shot as of late. He missed all five of his 3-point attempts Sunday night against the Celtics, and then went 6 of 7 against the Kings in the first half, tying a season high for an entire game.

Melton made four of the Warriors' nine threes in the first quarter. The veteran combo guard then tacked on another two threes in the second quarter, and missed his only attempt in the third. The first time he fired from downtown in the fourth quarter might have been his most difficult attempt of the night, and it still swished through the net.

Scoring bursts always are an added bonus for Melton. Needing to find his way out of a funk, Melton's strong start propelled the Warriors to another home win.
